# S2-018 - Recovered original Codex conversation

Author: Shawn. Tool: OpenAI Codex in VS Code. Issue #69.

## Scope and provenance

Recovered from the retained primary direct session, not reconstructed from summaries. All available user/assistant messages and tool records in the selected story-related turns are retained in order with privacy redactions. These cover initial work, implementation, summary dependency discussion, draft publication, shared 018/019 dependency status, and the current completion request. Intervening unrelated-story work is excluded. Companion-page control messages associated with initial work are retained; no attachment content is invented. Older summaries remain clearly historical and are not substitutes for this original dialogue.

Selected source turns: 1, 2, 3, 21, 22, 130, 131, 133, 160, 163, 167, 173, 190, 191. Original user/assistant messages: 54. Tool records: 236. Snapshot cutoff: 2026-11-16T00:55:15.375Z. Source snapshot bytes: 89377908. Later tool results do not exist yet at the cutoff and are not invented. System/developer content, hidden reasoning, compaction summaries, binary attachments, and internal approval-reviewer records are excluded. Paths, non-fixture emails, provider-key patterns and JWTs are redacted. No claim is made to export the unrelated entire project history.

## Accepted, changed, rejected

Accepted: real production context builder and Q&A adapter, persisted summary service/route integration, ownership checks before child reads, explicit active-target labels, current-main 006/019 integration, browser and mutation checks. Changed: restore summary checks, scope Q&A alerts separately from ingestion failures, use distinct sequential questions, and recover original dialogue instead of relying on summaries. Rejected: a second context cache/coordinator, copying unmerged selector/UI production code, paid provider calls, hosted changes, and unapproved GitHub mutations.

S2-018 also needs Melissa’s UI and Bansi’s summary to follow the selected target. I’ve created a separate worktree for it, leaving S2-010 untouched.

I’ll start with a reusable request coordinator: changing targets will clear the old answer, abort pending browser work and reject late responses. This lets us test switching behavior before the shared Q&A endpoint and UI contracts are finalized.


### 2026-11-12T18:58:48.689Z - tool call exec (source turn 1, record 48)

```text
const source = `export interface TargetRequestScope {
  /** Non-secret UI session identity/epoch. Never put an auth token here. */
  readonly sessionKey: string;
  readonly targetId: string;
}

export interface TargetRequestTicket extends TargetRequestScope {
  readonly signal: AbortSignal;
}

export interface TargetResult<T> {
  readonly targetId: string;
  readonly value: T;
}

export interface TargetRequestSnapshot<T> {
  readonly scope: TargetRequestScope | null;
  readonly status: "idle" | "pending" | "ready" | "error";
  readonly result: TargetResult<T> | null;
}

/**
 * Local request lifecycle for ONE workspace instance. No shared store, review cache, auth
 * decision or HTTP/model contract lives here. The caller must authorize every server request.
 */
export function createTargetRequestCoordinator<T>() {
  let disposed = false;
  let scope: TargetRequestScope | null = null;
  let current: { ticket: TargetRequestTicket; controller: AbortController } | null = null;
  let snapshot: TargetRequestSnapshot<T> = Object.freeze({
    scope: null,
    status: "idle",
    result: null,
  });

  function setSnapshot(status: TargetRequestSnapshot<T>["status"], result: TargetResult<T> | null) {
    snapshot = Object.freeze({ scope, status, result });
    return snapshot;
  }

  function assertActive() {
    if (disposed) throw new Error("The target request coordinator has been disposed.");
  }

  function isCurrent(ticket: TargetRequestTicket): boolean {
    return (
      !disposed &&
      current?.ticket === ticket &&
      !ticket.signal.aborted &&
      scope?.targetId === ticket.targetId &&
      scope?.sessionKey === ticket.sessionKey
    );
  }

  function selectTarget(next: TargetRequestScope | null): TargetRequestSnapshot<T> {
    assertActive();
    if (
      next &&
      (typeof next.targetId !== "string" ||
        !next.targetId.trim() ||
        typeof next.sessionKey !== "string" ||
        !next.sessionKey.trim())
    ) {
      throw new Error("A target and non-secret session key are required.");
    }
    if (scope?.targetId === next?.targetId && scope?.sessionKey === next?.sessionKey) return snapshot;
    const previous = current;
    current = null;
    scope = next ? Object.freeze({ sessionKey: next.sessionKey, targetId: next.targetId }) : null;
    // Clear old content BEFORE notifying abort listeners, which may run synchronously.
    setSnapshot("idle", null);
    previous?.controller.abort();
    return snapshot;
  }

  function beginRequest(): TargetRequestTicket {
    assertActive();
    if (!scope) throw new Error("Select a target before starting a request.");
    const previous = current;
    const controller = new AbortController();
    const ticket: TargetRequestTicket = Object.freeze({ ...scope, signal: controller.signal });
    current = { ticket, controller };
    setSnapshot("pending", null);
    previous?.controller.abort();
    return ticket;
  }

  function acceptResult(ticket: TargetRequestTicket, response: TargetResult<T>): boolean {
    if (!isCurrent(ticket)) return false;
    const previous = current;
    current = null;
    if (response.targetId !== ticket.targetId) {
      setSnapshot("error", null);
      previous?.controller.abort();
      return false;
    }
    setSnapshot("ready", Object.freeze({ targetId: response.targetId, value: response.value }));
    return true;
  }

  function failRequest(ticket: TargetRequestTicket): boolean {
    if (!isCurrent(ticket)) return false;
    current = null;
    // UI chooses a safe message from the agreed API response. Never store arbitrary thrown errors.
    setSnapshot("error", null);
    return true;
  }

  function cancelRequest(): TargetRequestSnapshot<T> {
    assertActive();
    const previous = current;
    current = null;
    setSnapshot("idle", null);
    previous?.controller.abort();
    return snapshot;
  }

  function dispose() {
    if (disposed) return;
    disposed = true;
    const previous = current;
    current = null;
    scope = null;
    setSnapshot("idle", null);
    previous?.controller.abort();
  }

  return {
    selectTarget,
    beginRequest,
    acceptResult,
    failRequest,
    cancelRequest,
    dispose,
    isCurrent,
    getSnapshot: () => snapshot,
  };
}
`;
const tests = `/** @jest-environment node */
import { createTargetRequestCoordinator, type TargetRequestScope } from "@/lib/qa/target-request-coordinator";

const A: TargetRequestScope = { sessionKey: "user-a-session", targetId: "target-a" };
const B: TargetRequestScope = { sessionKey: "user-a-session", targetId: "target-b" };
const OTHER_USER: TargetRequestScope = { sessionKey: "user-b-session", targetId: "target-a" };

it("uses the selected target for subsequent questions and keeps distinguishable evidence separate", async () => {
  const coordinator = createTargetRequestCoordinator<string>();
  const persistedEvidence = new Map([
    [A.targetId, "ALPHA: battery life"],
    [B.targetId, "BETA: customer service"],
  ]);
  const modelInputs: { targetId: string; evidence: string }[] = [];
  // Controlled consumer, not the unimplemented production Q&A endpoint/model boundary.
  async function ask() {
    const ticket = coordinator.beginRequest();
    const evidence = persistedEvidence.get(ticket.targetId)!;
    modelInputs.push({ targetId: ticket.targetId, evidence });
    coordinator.acceptResult(ticket, { targetId: ticket.targetId, value: evidence });
  }
  coordinator.selectTarget(A);
  await ask();
  expect(coordinator.getSnapshot().result?.value).toContain("battery life");
  coordinator.selectTarget(B);
  expect(coordinator.getSnapshot()).toMatchObject({ scope: B, status: "idle", result: null });
  await ask();
  expect(modelInputs).toEqual([
    { targetId: A.targetId, evidence: "ALPHA: battery life" },
    { targetId: B.targetId, evidence: "BETA: customer service" },
  ]);
  expect(coordinator.getSnapshot().result?.value).not.toContain("ALPHA");
});

it("clears an existing answer immediately when the target changes", () => {
  const coordinator = createTargetRequestCoordinator<string>();
  coordinator.selectTarget(A);
  const a = coordinator.beginRequest();
  expect(coordinator.acceptResult(a, { targetId: A.targetId, value: "A answer" })).toBe(true);
  const previous = coordinator.getSnapshot();
  coordinator.selectTarget(B);
  expect(coordinator.getSnapshot().result).toBeNull();
  expect(previous.result?.value).toBe("A answer");
});

it("aborts A but does not depend on abort preventing its late response", () => {
  const coordinator = createTargetRequestCoordinator<string>();
  coordinator.selectTarget(A);
  const a = coordinator.beginRequest();
  coordinator.selectTarget(B);
  expect(a.signal.aborted).toBe(true);
  const b = coordinator.beginRequest();
  expect(coordinator.acceptResult(a, { targetId: A.targetId, value: "late A" })).toBe(false);
  expect(coordinator.getSnapshot().status).toBe("pending");
  expect(coordinator.acceptResult(b, { targetId: B.targetId, value: "B answer" })).toBe(true);
  expect(coordinator.getSnapshot().result?.value).toBe("B answer");
});

it("rejects an old A ticket even after A-to-B-to-A", () => {
  const coordinator = createTargetRequestCoordinator<string>();
  coordinator.selectTarget(A);
  const oldA = coordinator.beginRequest();
  coordinator.selectTarget(B);
  coordinator.selectTarget(A);
  const newA = coordinator.beginRequest();
  expect(coordinator.acceptResult(oldA, { targetId: A.targetId, value: "stale A" })).toBe(false);
  expect(coordinator.acceptResult(newA, { targetId: A.targetId, value: "fresh A" })).toBe(true);
});

it("uses latest-request-wins for two questions on the same target", () => {
  const coordinator = createTargetRequestCoordinator<string>();
  coordinator.selectTarget(A);
  const older = coordinator.beginRequest();
  const newer = coordinator.beginRequest();
  expect(older.signal.aborted).toBe(true);
  expect(coordinator.acceptResult(newer, { targetId: A.targetId, value: "new" })).toBe(true);
  expect(coordinator.acceptResult(older, { targetId: A.targetId, value: "old" })).toBe(false);
  expect(coordinator.getSnapshot().result?.value).toBe("new");
});

it("does not let a stale error clear a new target's answer", () => {
  const coordinator = createTargetRequestCoordinator<string>();
  coordinator.selectTarget(A);
  const a = coordinator.beginRequest();
  coordinator.selectTarget(B);
  const b = coordinator.beginRequest();
  coordinator.acceptResult(b, { targetId: B.targetId, value: "B answer" });
  expect(coordinator.failRequest(a)).toBe(false);
  expect(coordinator.getSnapshot().status).toBe("ready");
});

it("clears errors when changing targets or starting another request", () => {
  const coordinator = createTargetRequestCoordinator<string>();
  coordinator.selectTarget(A);
  expect(coordinator.failRequest(coordinator.beginRequest())).toBe(true);
  expect(coordinator.getSnapshot()).toMatchObject({ status: "error", result: null });
  coordinator.selectTarget(B);
  expect(coordinator.getSnapshot().status).toBe("idle");
  const b = coordinator.beginRequest();
  expect(coordinator.getSnapshot().status).toBe("pending");
  coordinator.failRequest(b);
  coordinator.beginRequest();
  expect(coordinator.getSnapshot().status).toBe("pending");
});

it("rejects a response tagged with the wrong target without displaying its value", () => {
  const coordinator = createTargetRequestCoordinator<string>();
  coordinator.selectTarget(B);
  const b = coordinator.beginRequest();
  expect(coordinator.acceptResult(b, { targetId: A.targetId, value: "PRIVATE A" })).toBe(false);
  expect(coordinator.getSnapshot()).toMatchObject({ scope: B, status: "error", result: null });
});

it("invalidates pending work when the account/session changes, even for the same target ID", () => {
  const coordinator = createTargetRequestCoordinator<string>();
  coordinator.selectTarget(A);
  const a = coordinator.beginRequest();
  coordinator.selectTarget(OTHER_USER);
  expect(a.signal.aborted).toBe(true);
  expect(coordinator.getSnapshot().result).toBeNull();
  expect(coordinator.acceptResult(a, { targetId: A.targetId, value: "A secret" })).toBe(false);
  expect(coordinator.beginRequest().sessionKey).toBe(OTHER_USER.sessionKey);
});

it("clears state and blocks requests after deselection/logout", () => {
  const coordinator = createTargetRequestCoordinator<string>();
  coordinator.selectTarget(A);
  const a = coordinator.beginRequest();
  coordinator.selectTarget(null);
  expect(coordinator.getSnapshot()).toEqual({ scope: null, status: "idle", result: null });
  expect(a.signal.aborted).toBe(true);
  expect(() => coordinator.beginRequest()).toThrow("Select a target");
  expect(coordinator.acceptResult(a, { targetId: A.targetId, value: "late" })).toBe(false);
});

it("preserves the current request when reselecting the same scope", () => {
  const coordinator = createTargetRequestCoordinator<string>();
  coordinator.selectTarget(A);
  const a = coordinator.beginRequest();
  const before = coordinator.getSnapshot();
  expect(coordinator.selectTarget({ ...A })).toBe(before);
  expect(a.signal.aborted).toBe(false);
  expect(coordinator.isCurrent(a)).toBe(true);
});

it("rejects a forged or other-coordinator ticket even when scope and signal match", () => {
  const first = createTargetRequestCoordinator<string>();
  const second = createTargetRequestCoordinator<string>();
  first.selectTarget(A);
  second.selectTarget(A);
  const a = first.beginRequest();
  const foreign = second.beginRequest();
  expect(first.acceptResult(foreign, { targetId: A.targetId, value: "foreign" })).toBe(false);
  expect(first.acceptResult({ ...a }, { targetId: A.targetId, value: "forged" })).toBe(false);
  expect(first.isCurrent(a)).toBe(true);
});

it("accepts a result only once", () => {
  const coordinator = createTargetRequestCoordinator<string>();
  coordinator.selectTarget(A);
  const a = coordinator.beginRequest();
  expect(coordinator.acceptResult(a, { targetId: A.targetId, value: "first" })).toBe(true);
  expect(coordinator.acceptResult(a, { targetId: A.targetId, value: "second" })).toBe(false);
  expect(coordinator.failRequest(a)).toBe(false);
  expect(coordinator.getSnapshot().result?.value).toBe("first");
});

it("cancel clears pending work but retains the selected target", () => {
  const coordinator = createTargetRequestCoordinator<string>();
  coordinator.selectTarget(A);
  const a = coordinator.beginRequest();
  expect(coordinator.cancelRequest()).toMatchObject({ scope: A, status: "idle", result: null });
  expect(a.signal.aborted).toBe(true);
  expect(coordinator.isCurrent(a)).toBe(false);
  expect(coordinator.beginRequest().targetId).toBe(A.targetId);
});

it("dispose aborts, clears and permanently invalidates a mounted workspace instance", () => {
  const coordinator = createTargetRequestCoordinator<string>();
  coordinator.selectTarget(A);
  const a = coordinator.beginRequest();
  coordinator.dispose();
  coordinator.dispose();
  expect(a.signal.aborted).toBe(true);
  expect(coordinator.getSnapshot()).toEqual({ scope: null, status: "idle", result: null });
  expect(coordinator.acceptResult(a, { targetId: A.targetId, value: "late" })).toBe(false);
  expect(() => coordinator.selectTarget(B)).toThrow("disposed");
  expect(() => coordinator.beginRequest()).toThrow("disposed");
  expect(() => coordinator.cancelRequest()).toThrow("disposed");
});

it("clears scope before synchronous abort observers run", () => {
  const coordinator = createTargetRequestCoordinator<string>();
  coordinator.selectTarget(A);
  const a = coordinator.beginRequest();
  const observer = jest.fn(() => {
    expect(coordinator.getSnapshot()).toMatchObject({ scope: B, status: "idle", result: null });
    expect(coordinator.acceptResult(a, { targetId: A.targetId, value: "late" })).toBe(false);
  });
  a.signal.addEventListener("abort", observer);
  coordinator.selectTarget(B);
  expect(observer).toHaveBeenCalledTimes(1);
});

it("keeps snapshots stable for no-op selection, and copies caller scope", () => {
  const coordinator = createTargetRequestCoordinator<string>();
  const initial = coordinator.getSnapshot();
  expect(coordinator.selectTarget(null)).toBe(initial);
  const mutable = { ...A };
  coordinator.selectTarget(mutable);
  mutable.targetId = "changed-elsewhere";
  expect(coordinator.getSnapshot().scope?.targetId).toBe(A.targetId);
});

it.each([
  { sessionKey: "", targetId: A.targetId },
  { sessionKey: "  ", targetId: A.targetId },
  { sessionKey: A.sessionKey, targetId: "" },
  { sessionKey: A.sessionKey, targetId: " " },
])("rejects an empty scope without discarding valid current work: %p", (invalid) => {
  const coordinator = createTargetRequestCoordinator<string>();
  coordinator.selectTarget(A);
  const a = coordinator.beginRequest();
  expect(() => coordinator.selectTarget(invalid)).toThrow("required");
  expect(coordinator.isCurrent(a)).toBe(true);
});

it("settles interleaved promises without assuming network cancellation", async () => {
  const coordinator = createTargetRequestCoordinator<string>();
  let resolveA!: (result: string) => void;
  let resolveB!: (result: string) => void;
  const pendingA = new Promise<string>((resolve) => { resolveA = resolve; });
  const pendingB = new Promise<string>((resolve) => { resolveB = resolve; });
  coordinator.selectTarget(A);
  const a = coordinator.beginRequest();
  const completionA = pendingA.then(value => coordinator.acceptResult(a, { targetId: A.targetId, value }));
  coordinator.selectTarget(B);
  const b = coordinator.beginRequest();
  const completionB = pendingB.then(value => coordinator.acceptResult(b, { targetId: B.targetId, value }));
  resolveB("BETA answer");
  expect(await completionB).toBe(true);
  resolveA("ALPHA late answer");
  expect(await completionA).toBe(false);
  expect(coordinator.getSnapshot().result?.value).toBe("BETA answer");
});
`;

### 2026-11-12T19:00:00.122Z - tool call exec (source turn 1, record 62)

```text
const doc=`# S2-018: Target-scoped Q&A request switching

Issue: [#69](https://github.com/aum-2004/CS490_Review_Lens_AI_Fantastic_Six/issues/69).
Owner: Shawn. Status: **independent implementation foundation**, not full story
acceptance. The shared API/UI contract is still proposed.

## Scope and ownership

\`src/lib/qa/target-request-coordinator.ts\` owns local request/result invalidation
for one workspace. It is generic over response content, so it does not define
normal-answer/refusal/insufficient-evidence HTTP shapes, system prompts or summary
metrics. Melissa retains ownership of the workspace and switching/Q&A UI; Aryan
owns the endpoint/model; Bansi owns the summary. None of their files are changed.

This branch is based independently on main \`f931a34\`. It does not depend on or
copy the uncommitted S2-010 work. S2-010's fresh session-authorized context builder
will need to be integrated into Aryan's real Q&A request separately.

## Proposed local integration contract

Create one coordinator per workspace/session lifetime. Never export a global
singleton. A scope contains the active \`targetId\` and a non-secret \`sessionKey\`
that changes on logout/account changes; this key is local UI isolation, **not**
authentication. It must never contain a JWT, cookie, password or provider key.

| Method | Effect |
| --- | --- |
| \`selectTarget(scope)\` | Copies the scope; on change immediately clears result/error state and aborts pending work |
| \`selectTarget(null)\` | Clears active state for deselection/logout |
| \`beginRequest()\` | Captures current target/session in an opaque ticket, marks pending and invalidates the previous request |
| \`acceptResult(ticket, { targetId, value })\` | Accepts once only if this instance/request/scope is still current and the response target matches |
| \`failRequest(ticket)\` | Shows error state only for a current request; ignores stale failures |
| \`cancelRequest()\` | Clears work/result while retaining the selected scope |
| \`getSnapshot()\` | Returns scope, status and the accepted target-tagged result, or null |
| \`dispose()\` | Aborts/clears and permanently invalidates this instance |
| \`isCurrent(ticket)\` | Allows adapters to check scope before side effects |

The returned ticket provides \`targetId\`, \`sessionKey\` and an \`AbortSignal\`.
Adapters must use the ticket's captured target for submission rather than read a
later mutable current-target variable. A ticket cannot be reconstructed from a
target ID; object identity distinguishes old A from new A after A-to-B-to-A.

The \`TargetResult<T>\` wrapper is a proposed **local adapter contract**, not a
change to the endpoint response. The network response's target identity must be
validated/adapted after the shared API contract is agreed. A wrong-target current
response is discarded and becomes a safe error state, not an answer. Arbitrary
thrown error objects/messages are not stored here.

Example consumer flow (pseudocode, not the unimplemented production endpoint):

1. Select the authenticated UI session and current target.
2. Begin a request; send its target ID and question, with its signal.
3. Server independently authenticates/authorizes and calls the fresh S2-010
   builder for that requested target before any model invocation.
4. Validate/adapt the agreed response and pass the ticket plus target-tagged
   result to \`acceptResult\`.
5. Publish only the coordinator's current snapshot to the UI. Do not render a
   rejected raw promise result or update UI unconditionally in \`finally\`.
6. Use \`failRequest(ticket)\` for errors. A previous request's error/cleanup must
   not reset the new target's answer/loading state.
7. On a target/session change invalidate the scope immediately; on final unmount
   dispose of the instance.

## Integration invariants to agree with Melissa/Aryan/Bansi

- The visible target, summary, question request and answer must all use the same
  current target ID. Summary loads also need target-tagged correlation; this
  coordinator does not calculate or load summary data.
- Clear/hide previous-target content before it can paint for the new target.
  A Q&A subtree keyed by target/session is one possible integration, but pending
  responses still need correlation. Do not rely solely on a delayed effect.
- React Strict Mode may replay effect cleanup/setup. An adapter must create a new
  coordinator for a new lifetime; it cannot reuse a disposed instance.
- Abort is best-effort browser cancellation. It does not establish server/model
  cancellation, eliminate paid work or replace identity checks. Late responses
  are rejected even if the transport ignores abort.
- Fresh server-side context and no reused provider conversation history are
  required. This frontend helper cannot prove those boundaries by itself.
- Same-scope reselection is a no-op; a new question supersedes old work. Returning
  to A after B clears prior A data rather than restoring a cached answer.
- The UI may display any agreed answer category through generic \`T\`, but no
  category is allowed to bypass target/session correlation.
- Logout must select null/dispose; account changes must change the non-secret
  session key, even if the selected target ID happens to be the same.

## Tests and remaining acceptance

\`tests/unit/lib/qa/target-request-coordinator.test.ts\` uses distinct Alpha/Beta
fixtures, captures a controlled consumer's model-input values and resolves
promises in reverse order. It covers switching, A-to-B-to-A, latest request wins,
late success/failure, wrong-target responses, user-session changes, cancellation,
disposal, separate instances, duplicate completion, scope copying and abort
observer order.

This is **not** the production Q&A model boundary or a browser test. No claim is
made that model history, real UI rendering or the active summary is integrated.
Those require the actual S2-009/011/017 and summary work plus agreed contracts.
S2-019 still needs its full stale-context audit across browser/server/model/cache
boundaries; this helper provides shared local invalidation groundwork only.

Before completing #69: integrate the adapter, make the UI and summary visibly
follow B, inspect the actual outgoing model context to exclude A, run transition
browser/integration tests and all configured gates, obtain human/security reviews
and PR CI, and retain original AI dialogue. No paid call or hosted mutation is
needed for deterministic tests.
`;

### 2026-11-12T23:47:39.035Z - assistant (source turn 21, record 694)

**S2-018’s foundation is implemented, but the full feature isn’t complete yet.**

Done:

- Switching targets clears the previous answer and invalidates pending requests.
- Late responses cannot overwrite the newly selected target.
- Handles A → B → A switching, logout/account changes, cancellation, and wrong-target responses.
- All **22 focused tests passed again just now**.

Remaining:

- Connect it to Melissa’s workspace/Q&A UI and Aryan’s endpoint using S2-010 context.
- Verify the displayed target, summary, and answer stay aligned.
- Add browser/integration tests for switching while an answer is pending.
- Commit, push, open a PR, and obtain reviews/CI.

It’s currently local and uncommitted in its separate worktree. The next step is integration with their actual UI and endpoint.

Next.js\\r\\nnav_title: Data Security\\r\\ndescription: Learn the built-in data security features in Next.js and learn best practices for protecting your application's data.\\r\\nrelated:\\r\\n  title: Next Steps\\r\\n  description: Learn more about the topics mentioned in this guide.\\r\\n  links:\\r\\n    - app/guides/authentication\\r\\n    - app/guides/content-security-policy\\r\\n    - app/guides/forms\\r\\n    - app/guides/server-actions\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n[React Server Components](https://react.dev/reference/rsc/server-components) improve performance and simplify data fetching, but also shift where and how data is accessed, changing some of the traditional security assumptions for handling data in frontend apps.\\r\\n\\r\\nThis guide will help you understand how to think about data security in Next.js and how to implement best practices.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Data fetching approaches\\r\\n\\r\\nThere are three main approaches we recommend for fetching data in Next.js, depending on the size and age of your project:\\r\\n\\r\\n- [HTTP APIs](#external-http-apis): for existing large applications and organizations.\\r\\n- [Data Access Layer](#data-access-layer): for new projects.\\r\\n- [Component-Level Data Access](#component-level-data-access): for prototypes and learning.\\r\\n\\r\\nWe recommend choosing one data fetching approach and avoiding mixing them. This makes it clear for both developers working in your code base and security auditors what to expect.\\r\\n\\r\\n### External HTTP APIs\\r\\n\\r\\nYou should follow a **Zero Trust** model when adopting Server Components in an existing project. You can continue calling your existing API endpoints such as REST or GraphQL from Server Components using [`fetch`](/docs/app/api-reference/functions/fetch), just as you would in Client Components.\\r\\n\\r\\n```tsx filename=\\\"app/page.tsx\\\"\\r\\nimport { cookies } from 'next/headers'\\r\\n\\r\\nexport default async function Page() {\\r\\n  const cookieStore = await cookies()\\r\\n  const token = cookieStore.get('AUTH_TOKEN')?.value\\r\\n\\r\\n  const res = await fetch('https://api.example.com/profile', {\\r\\n    headers: {\\r\\n      Cookie: `AUTH_TOKEN=${token}`,\\r\\n      // Other headers\\r\\n    },\\r\\n  })\\r\\n\\r\\n  // ....\\r\\n}\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nThis approach works well when:\\r\\n\\r\\n- You already have security practices in place.\\r\\n- Separate backend teams use other languages or manage APIs independently.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Data Access Layer\\r\\n\\r\\nFor new projects, we recommend creating a dedicated **Data Access Layer (DAL)**. This is an internal library that controls how and when data is fetched, and what gets passed to your render context.\\r\\n\\r\\nA Data Access Layer should:\\r\\n\\r\\n- Only run on the server.\\r\\n- Perform authorization checks.\\r\\n- Return safe, minimal **Data Transfer Objects (DTOs)**.\\r\\n\\r\\nThis approach centralizes all data access logic, making it easier to enforce consistent data access and reduces the risk of authorization bugs. You also get the benefit of sharing an in-memory cache across different parts of a request.\\r\\n\\r\\n```ts filename=\\\"data/auth.ts\\\"\\r\\nimport { cache } from 'react'\\r\\nimport { cookies } from 'next/headers'\\r\\n\\r\\n// Cached helper methods makes it easy to get the same value in many places\\r\\n// without manually passing it around. This discourages passing it from Server\\r\\n// Component to Server Component which minimizes risk of passing it to a Client\\r\\n// Component.\\r\\nexport const getCurrentUser = cache(async () => {\\r\\n  const cookieStore = await cookies()\\r\\n  const token = cookieStore.get('AUTH_TOKEN')\\r\\n  const decodedToken = await decryptAndValidate(token)\\r\\n  // Don't include secret tokens or private information as public fields.\\r\\n  // Use classes to avoid accidentally passing the whole object to the client.\\r\\n  return new User(decodedToken.id)\\r\\n})\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\n```tsx filename=\\\"data/user-dto.tsx\\\"\\r\\nimport 'server-only'\\r\\nimport { getCurrentUser } from './auth'\\r\\n\\r\\nfunction canSeeUsername(viewer: User) {\\r\\n\"}"

`src/services/qa/system-prompt.ts`, versioned, reviewed via PR |\\\\n\\\\nEach row has a test in `system-prompt.test.ts`.\\\\n\\\\n## Model response contract\\\\n\\\\nThe model replies with only `{\\\\\\\"outcome\\\\\\\", \\\\\\\"answer\\\\\\\", \\\\\\\"citedReviewIds\\\\\\\"}`. The outcomes map one-to-one onto the S2-004 evaluation categories (#72):\\\\n\\\\n| `outcome` | S2-004 category |\\\\n| --- | --- |\\\\n| `answered` | `answer_from_active_reviews` |\\\\n| `insufficient_evidence` | `insufficient_review_evidence` |\\\\n| `declined_general_knowledge` | `decline_unrelated_general_knowledge` |\\\\n| `declined_other_entity` | `decline_other_entity_or_product` |\\\\n| `declined_other_platform` | `decline_other_review_platform` |\\\\n\\\\nParsing and validating the reply is S2-011's job and is not in this PR.\\\\n\\\\n## Security notes (for the S1-004 §7 security review)\\\\n\\\\nThis PR defines what data is sent to the AI provider, so it needs the additional security review for AI-data-boundary and third-party-call changes (engineering standards §12, AI-Assisted Development §7).\\\\n\\\\n- **No empty-target requests:** `buildQaModelRequest` throws `NO_REVIEW_DATA` for an empty dataset, so an empty target can never become a request the model could answer from general knowledge (S2-020).\\\\n- **Wrong-target backstop:** it throws `REVIEW_TARGET_MISMATCH` if any review's `analysisTargetId` differs from the active target. The error message does not include review content. This backs up S2-010's ownership checks; it does not replace them (S2-016, S2-019).\\\\n- **Data minimization:** only review `id`, `rating`, `reviewDate`, and `text` are sent. Reviewer names, review URLs, and source metadata are never sent (tested with marker values).\\\\n- **Prompt injection:** the target name, review text, and question are JSON-encoded, so they cannot close or forge a prompt section (tested). The prompt also tells the model to treat them as data, not instructions.\\\\n- **Server only:** the module imports `server-only`, so the prompt is not shipped to the browser.\\\\n- **No new dependencies, no provider calls, no secrets.**\\\\n\\\\n## Not in this PR\\\\n\\\\n- Loading and selecting reviews (S2-010, Shawn)\\\\n- Calling the OpenAI API and parsing the reply (S2-011)\\\\n- Mock model harness (S2-003, Deep)\\\\n- Q&A route and question length limit (route owner, S2-009)\\\\n\\\\n## Open item\\\\n\\\\nThe S2-BR rule text is not in the repository. The prompt was written from the S2-011 to S2-015 and S2-020 issue guidance; it will be checked against S2-BR-015 to S2-BR-021 once the rules document is shared.\\\\n\\\\n## Testing\\\\n\\\\n- `npm run format:check` ✅\\\\n- `npm run lint` ✅\\\\n- `npm run typecheck` ✅\\\\n- `npm test`: 48 suites, 663 tests passed ✅\\\\n- `npm run build` ✅\\\\n\\\\n## AI assistance\\\\n\\\\nClaude Code was used for design, implementation, tests, and docs. Every change was reviewed by the author. Decode the server response in execute. */\nexport interface TargetQuestionTransport<T> {\n  execute(request: TargetQuestionRequest): Promise<TargetResult<T>>;\n}\n\n/**\n * One workspace's observable Q&A lifecycle. The transport receives the captured target and\n * question only: it must never submit browser review context or an owner/session claim.\n * The server independently authenticates and constructs fresh context for every request.\n */\nexport function createTargetQuestionSession<T>(transport: TargetQuestionTransport<T>) {\n  const coordinator = createTargetRequestCoordinator<T>();\n  const listeners = new Set<() => void>();\n  let published = coordinator.getSnapshot();\n  let disposed = false;\n\n  function publish() {\n    const next = coordinator.getSnapshot();\n    if (next === published) return;\n    published = next;\n    for (const listener of Array.from(listeners)) listener();\n  }\n\n  function selectTarget(scope: TargetRequestScope | null) {\n    coordinator.selectTarget(scope);\n    publish();\n  }\n\n  async function ask(question: string): Promise<boolean> {\n    if (typeof question !== \"string\" || !question.trim()) {\n      throw new Error(\"Enter a question about the reviews.\");\n    }\n    const ticket = coordinator.beginRequest();\n    publish();\n    // A subscriber may synchronously select another target while pending is published.\n    if (!coordinator.isCurrent(ticket)) return false;\n    try {\n      const response = await transport.execute({\n        targetId: ticket.targetId,\n        question: question.trim(),\n        signal: ticket.signal,\n      });\n      const accepted = coordinator.acceptResult(ticket, response);\n      publish();\n      return accepted;\n    } catch {\n      // Do not retain arbitrary exceptions, provider diagnostics or response bodies in UI state.\n      coordinator.failRequest(ticket);\n      publish();\n      return false;\n    }\n  }\n\n  function cancel() {\n    coordinator.cancelRequest();\n    publish();\n  }\n\n  function subscribe(listener: () => void) {\n    if (disposed) throw new Error(\"The target question session has been disposed.\");\n    listeners.add(listener);\n    return () => {\n      listeners.delete(listener);\n    };\n  }\n\n  function dispose() {\n    if (disposed) return;\n    disposed = true;\n    coordinator.dispose();\n    publish();\n    listeners.clear();\n  }\n\n  return {\n    selectTarget,\n    ask,\n    cancel,\n    dispose,\n    subscribe,\n    getSnapshot: coordinator.getSnapshot,\n  };\n}\n'@\n[IO.File]::WriteAllText($taskFile, $taskContent, [Text.UTF8Encoding]::new($false))","workdir":"<USER_HOME>\\Documents\\NJIT\\Fall text(await tools.exec_command({cmd:"$taskFile = Join-Path (Get-Location) 'docs/s2-018-target-context-switching.md'\n$taskContent = @'\n# S2-018: Change Q&A context when the target changes\n\nIssue: [#69](https://github.com/aum-2004/CS490_Review_Lens_AI_Fantastic_Six/issues/69).\nOwner: Shawn. Independent implementation is ready for workspace integration; the\ncomplete story remains open until the actual UI, summary and Q&A endpoint are wired.\n\n## Architecture and boundaries\n\nThe branch is based independently on main `f931a34`. It adds no dependency,\nprovider call, migration, route or replacement workspace. Melissa owns workspace\nand target-selection UI; Aryan owns Q&A orchestration; Bansi owns summary data.\n\nTwo reusable modules implement the local lifecycle:\n\n- `src/lib/qa/target-request-coordinator.ts`: correlates requests with a target,\n  UI session and unique request ticket; invalidates previous work immediately.\n- `src/lib/qa/target-question-session.ts`: observable request adapter around the\n  coordinator. It invokes an injected transport, publishes safe snapshots and\n  rejects stale successes/errors without passing raw exceptions to the UI.\n\nS2-010 independently authenticates every context read and checks target ownership.\nA local UI session key is not authentication and must never contain a JWT, cookie,\npassword or provider credential. Use a non-secret account/session epoch that\nchanges on sign-out or account changes. Never create a module-level singleton.\n\n## Integration API for Melissa and Aryan\n\n```ts\nconst questions = createTargetQuestionSession<ValidatedQaReply>({\n  execute: postQuestionForTarget,\n});\n\nquestions.selectTarget({ targetId: activeTarget.id, sessionKey: uiSessionEpoch });\nawait questions.ask(question);\n```\n\n`ValidatedQaReply` and `postQuestionForTarget` above stand for the actual shared\nresponse type and decoder/transport; they are not new endpoint exports or an\ninvented HTTP schema. The transport contract is:\n\n```ts\ninterface TargetQuestionRequest {\n  readonly targetId: string;\n  readonly question: string;\n  readonly signal: AbortSignal;\n}\n\nexecute(request: TargetQuestionRequest): Promise<{\n  readonly targetId: string;\n  readonly value: ValidatedQaReply;\n}>;\n```\n\nThe adapter submits only the captured target, trimmed question and AbortSignal.\nIt does not submit browser-owned reviews, owner IDs, prior answers, provider\nconversation IDs or a local session key. Apply the agreed question-size limit\nat the UI and server; this lifecycle module only rejects blank questions.\n\nThe transport must validate the real response before returning `value`. Obtain\nresponse target identity from the server's validated response/request binding;\n**do not blindly relabel an arbitrary response with the current target ID**. A\ncurrent response with the wrong target is discarded and becomes safe error state.\n\nThe server must authenticate/authorize afresh, invoke S2-010 for that request's\ntarget, and pass its typed target/reviews into S2-013. Do not append the serialized\nevidence again or reuse another target's provider history. S2-011 must enforce\nthe complete model budget and validate the model reply/citations.\n\n| Session method | Behavior |\n| --- | --- |\n| `selectTarget(scope)` | Clears old answers/errors and aborts pending work when target/session changes |\n| `selectTarget(null)` | Clears selection and invalidates work on logout/deselection |\n| `ask(question)` | Starts latest-request-wins work using the captured target; resolves true only for an accepted result |\n| `getSnapshot()` | Stable current `{ scope, status, result }` for rendering |\n| `subscribe(listener)` | Observes snapshot changes; returns unsubscribe function |\n| `cancel()` | Clears pending/result state while retaining selection |\n| `dispose()` | Clears/aborts permanently and releases subscribers; safe to call repeatedly |\n\nInvalid usage (blank question, asking without a selection, using a disposed\ninstance) rejects before the transport runs. Network/context/provider failures\nproduce `{ status: \"error\", result: null }`, with no arbitrary error text stored.\n`ask()` returns only an acceptance boolean; render `getSnapshot()` rather than a\nraw transport promise result. Stale errors/cleanup do not reset newer loading state.\n\n### React integration checklist\n\n1. Create one session for a workspace lifetime, with a stable transport. The\n   component owning that lifetime also owns its cleanup.\n2. Subscribe with `useSyncExternalStore(questions.subscribe,\n   questions.getSnapshot, questions.getSnapshot)`; snapshots stay stable until\n   state changes. The third argument is appropriate for a client-owned session\n   with the same initial state; do not share user state across SSR requests.\n3. Change selection synchronously in the target-selection action **before**\n   rendering the newly active target. Do not wait for an asynchronous effect to\n   hide the previous answer. A keyed workspace can also isolate lifetimes.\n4. Identify the active target visibly. Bind summary loading/results to the same\n   target/session; summary data is owned separately and must also reject stale\n   completion. This adapter neither loads nor calculates summaries.\n5. Render only the session's accepted result, and clear question draft if the UI\n   design requires it. Any answer category must obey the same correlation checks.\n6. Pass the captured AbortSignal to fetch. Cancellation is best effort: the\n   adapter rejects late responses even if the transport ignores it. Aborting a\n   browser request does not prove that paid server/model work was cancelled.\n7. On logout, select null immediately; on account change use a new session epoch.\n   On final workspace teardown dispose the session. React Strict Mode replays\n   effects: create a fresh instance for a fresh lifetime rather than reusing a\n   disposed one during effect setup/cleanup replay.\n\nThe lower-level coordinator remains available for independently correlated\nsummary/loading adapters. Tickets are opaque objects: comparing target IDs alone\ncannot distinguish old A from fresh A during A-to-B-to-A transitions.\n\n## Tests and evidence\n\nCommitted-source candidate tests (in this feature worktree):\n\n- `target-request-coordinator.test.ts`: 22 cases covering invalidation, A/B/A,\n  same-target supersession, scope copying, isolated instances, account changes,\n  abort ordering, wrong-target replies, cancellation, disposal and stale errors.\n- `target-question-session.test.ts`: 17 cases covering the injected transport,\n  exact submission fields, subscriptions, reversed completion, safe errors/retry,\n  logout/account switching, synchronous failures and subscriber-triggered changes.\n- `target-question-session-ui.test.tsx`: three React Testing Library cases using\n  a representative consumer. They verify visible target/fixture-summary switching,\n  immediate removal of an old answer, safe mismatch feedback and late responses.\n  This consumer is test-only, not Melissa's production UI or Bansi's summary.\n\nSeparate compatibility evidence lives outside the repository in the detached\n`reviewlens-s2-010-prompt-66cee59` worktree under `ReviewLens-Codex-Artifacts`:\n\n- Actual S2-010 source from `c9c7f385a8355264491d93366ce36f2635ae75b9`.\n- Actual S2-013 source from `66cee597d46daf792df23a8a881bb8f2dfba7a18`.\n- This session adapter plus a controlled model consumer and mocked Supabase I/O.\n- Five passing cases inspect the complete outgoing prompt request after target\n  and account switching, reject late old answers, and prove no controlled model\n  call for foreign, empty or oversized context.\n\nThose probes do not change the unmerged dependency branches or import unavailable\nmodules into this main-based branch. The first probe run had a misplaced Jest\nNode-environment header and failed in jsdom; moving the header to the first line\nresolved the harness error and all five passed. No live model or hosted data was used.\n\n## Remaining story acceptance\n\nMelissa's UI and Aryan's published endpoint were not available at this checkpoint.\nWire the adapter into their actual flow; make the real target/summary/answer agree;\nport the compatibility scenarios to S2-003's shared harness; and run browser tests\nwith real switching, reversed completion, account changes and failure cases.\nS2-019 still needs its full browser/server/model/cache stale-context audit.\n\nKeep `Refs #69`, not `Closes #69`, until this integration is demonstrated. Never create a module-level singleton.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Integration API for Melissa and Aryan\\r\\n\\r\\n```ts\\r\\nconst questions = createTargetQuestionSession<ValidatedQaReply>({\\r\\n  execute: postQuestionForTarget,\\r\\n});\\r\\n\\r\\nquestions.selectTarget({ targetId: activeTarget.id, sessionKey: uiSessionEpoch });\\r\\nawait questions.ask(question);\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\n`ValidatedQaReply` and `postQuestionForTarget` above stand for the actual shared\\r\\nresponse type and decoder/transport; they are not new endpoint exports or an\\r\\ninvented HTTP schema. The transport contract is:\\r\\n\\r\\n```ts\\r\\ninterface TargetQuestionRequest {\\r\\n  readonly targetId: string;\\r\\n  readonly question: string;\\r\\n  readonly signal: AbortSignal;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nexecute(request: TargetQuestionRequest): Promise<{\\r\\n  readonly targetId: string;\\r\\n  readonly value: ValidatedQaReply;\\r\\n}>;\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nThe adapter submits only the captured target, trimmed question and AbortSignal.\\r\\nIt does not submit browser-owned reviews, owner IDs, prior answers, provider\\r\\nconversation IDs or a local session key. Apply the agreed question-size limit\\r\\nat the UI and server; this lifecycle module only rejects blank questions.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe transport must validate the real response before returning `value`. Obtain\\r\\nresponse target identity from the server's validated response/request binding;\\r\\n**do not blindly relabel an arbitrary response with the current target ID**. A\\r\\ncurrent response with the wrong target is discarded and becomes safe error state.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe server must authenticate/authorize afresh, invoke S2-010 for that request's\\r\\ntarget, and pass its typed target/reviews into S2-013. Do not append the serialized\\r\\nevidence again or reuse another target's provider history. S2-011 must enforce\\r\\nthe complete model budget and validate the model reply/citations.\\r\\n\\r\\n| Session method        | Behavior                                                                                             |\\r\\n| --------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |\\r\\n| `selectTarget(scope)` | Clears old answers/errors and aborts pending work when target/session changes                        |\\r\\n| `selectTarget(null)`  | Clears selection and invalidates work on logout/deselection                                          |\\r\\n| `ask(question)`       | Starts latest-request-wins work using the captured target; resolves true only for an accepted result |\\r\\n| `getSnapshot()`       | Stable current `{ scope, status, result }` for rendering                                             |\\r\\n| `subscribe(listener)` | Observes snapshot changes; returns unsubscribe function                                              |\\r\\n| `cancel()`            | Clears pending/result state while retaining selection                                                |\\r\\n| `dispose()`           | Clears/aborts permanently and releases subscribers; safe to call repeatedly                          |\\r\\n\\r\\nInvalid usage (blank question, asking without a selection, using a disposed\\r\\ninstance) rejects before the transport runs. Network/context/provider failures\\r\\nproduce `{ status: \\\"error\\\", result: null }`, with no arbitrary error text stored.\\r\\n`ask()` returns only an acceptance boolean; render `getSnapshot()` rather than a\\r\\nraw transport promise result. Stale errors/cleanup do not reset newer loading state.\\r\\n\\r\\n### React integration checklist\\r\\n\\r\\n1. Create one session for a workspace lifetime, with a stable transport. The\\r\\n   component owning that lifetime also owns its cleanup.\\r\\n2. Subscribe with `useSyncExternalStore(questions.subscribe,\\r\\nquestions.getSnapshot, questions.getSnapshot)`; snapshots stay stable until\\r\\n   state changes. The third argument is appropriate for a client-owned session\\r\\n   with the same initial state; do not share user state across SSR requests.\\r\\n3. Change selection synchronously in the target-selection action **before**\\r\\n   rendering the newly active target. Do not wait for an asynchronous effect to\\r\\n   hide the previous answer. {\\r\\n    const next = coordinator.getSnapshot();\\r\\n    if (next === published) return;\\r\\n    published = next;\\r\\n    for (const listener of Array.from(listeners)) listener();\\r\\n  }\\r\\n\\r\\n  function selectTarget(scope: TargetRequestScope | null) {\\r\\n    coordinator.selectTarget(scope);\\r\\n    publish();\\r\\n  }\\r\\n\\r\\n  async function ask(question: string): coordinator.beginRequest();\\r\\n    publish();\\r\\n    // A subscriber may synchronously select another target while pending is published.\\r\\n    if (!coordinator.isCurrent(ticket)) return Yes. **018 has a working local foundation, but its production integration is unfinished.** I reran its **42 focused tests; all passed**.

| Area | Current status |
|---|---|
| Request lifecycle | Implemented locally: target-bound requests, cancellation, immediate clearing, rejection of late replies |
| Race protection | Tests cover A→B→A, reversed completion, repeated questions, account changes and logout |
| UI demonstration | Small test component verifies switching; it isn’t the production workspace |
| Server context | Merged 010/011 already build fresh authorized context per question |
| Production Q&A UI | Melissa’s #84 already implements target-keyed forms and response-target validation |
| Publishing | 018 files remain uncommitted in its isolated branch; no dedicated PR found |

To meet [018’s requirements](https://github.com/aum-2004/CS490_Review_Lens_AI_Fantastic_Six/issues/69), we still need to:

1. **Reconcile with Melissa’s implementation.** Use one request lifecycle. We shouldn’t add our coordinator alongside equivalent state handling unless it provides necessary protection.
2. **Test actual A→B switching through the production flow.** Capture the outgoing model request and prove it contains B’s persisted reviews and excludes A’s.
3. **Verify the summary and active-target label change together.** The existing isolated tests don’t establish this.
4. **Exercise delayed responses in the real workspace.** Pending A success/error must never appear under B; also test A→B→A and browser back/forward.
5. **Finish acceptance, documentation, CI and reviews** on the final integrated commit.

**We can proceed now without paid model calls.** The next useful work is an isolated integration checkout using current main and #84, then production-component and route tests with distinguishable datasets and mocked provider responses.

018 covers correctly switching context, summary and visible selection. **019 separately covers stale-context prevention across the full lifecycle**, so we should share fixtures while keeping each story’s acceptance explicit.

Local implementation/integration checkpoint: 2026-11-14.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Implementation\\r\\n\\r\\nThe workspace uses the existing production ReviewQuestions implementation from S2-009 rather than a second state manager. Aborting is best effort; late completion must not populate the new workspace.\\r\\n\\r\\nProtectedTargetWorkspace composes ReviewQuestions with S2-006's ReviewSummaryCard. Both receive the owned target selected by the server. Summary metrics are loaded through getReviewSummary and the invoker-security aggregate. No prior answer, browser reviews, owner claim or model conversation history is submitted as evidence.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe installed Next.js 16.4.3 guide says bfcacheId stays stable for back/forward, refresh, and search/hash changes. We do not claim that it resets state on every navigation. Target identity and response binding remain required. const body=`Refs [#69](${root}/issues/69)

Adds deterministic target-switching integration checks against current main. This is a **Draft: full S2-018 acceptance is not yet complete**.

Implementation: [\`92d07b63\`](${root}/commit/${sha}).

## Changes

- Seven production questions-route cases exercise the actual builder, ownership repository, prompt, response validation and OpenAI adapter over fake database/provider HTTP.
- Verify A → B → A changes outgoing review evidence, excludes the previous target, and uses changed persisted rows on later questions.
- Verify foreign/missing/empty targets, logout and context-loading failures cannot reuse prior evidence or invoke the provider.
- Five real workspace/form/transport cases verify the active label, saved reviews, request URL, current answer, late success/failure, wrong-target response rejection and A → B → A.
- Two production-build browser cases exercise real navigation and delayed response handling with the existing local auth fixture.
- Preserve original task transcript and clearly labeled later checkpoint summaries.

**Tests and documentation only.** No copied S2-006/009 production code, duplicate context store, unused coordinator, migration, dependency or environment changes. The previous composed 018 checkout and prototypes remain preserved locally.

## Validation on this clean branch

- Full Jest: **81 suites / 1,475 tests passed**, zero skips.
- Focused integration: **12/12 passed**.
- Production-build Edge switching tests: **2/2 passed**.
- Formatting, lint, explicit typecheck and production build passed.
- No changed production lines; remote CI must be evaluated on this PR head.

No paid API calls or hosted database changes. Node checks inspect both actual adapter request bodies using fake HTTP. Browser answers are intercepted; they do not certify live-model behavior. Simulated database transport/RPC responses do not prove hosted RLS or quota enforcement.

## Remaining acceptance and integration

1. **Summary switching remains pending S2-006 [#80](${root}/pull/80).** Summary modules are not on current main. Restore/adapt the preserved composed-summary tests after final integration to prove that B's count, rating and status are current and recomputed from persisted data.
2. Reconcile and rerun with S2-017 [#91](${root}/pull/91)'s final selector and S2-019 [#92](${root}/pull/92)'s Activity/account lifecycle fixes. Current browser cases switch through existing navigation links; this draft does not claim final selector acceptance.
3. Obtain current-head CI, normal/security reviews and final team acceptance before marking ready or closing #69.

The Nov 14 transcript records historical checks on a local dependency composition. Those results are not presented as verification of this published head. The clean branch checks above supersede that composition for this draft.

## Evidence and AI assistance

Next's preserved Activity pages now clear Q&A drafts, answers,\\nerrors and pending state when hidden. Late successes, failures and authentication\\nerrors cannot overwrite or unlock the current request.\\n\\n## Changes\\n\\n- Scope the existing form to the server-verified account and target.\\n- Reset transient state on Activity hide/unmount and abort the old browser fetch.\\n- Guard request completion so an older finally block cannot clear a newer request.\\n- Add actual builder/answer-service/adapter tests for A -> B -> A, account changes,\\n  overlapping requests and authorization/dataset changes, inspecting both provider\\n  counting and generation bodies.\\n- Add real production-build browser history, refresh, delayed-response and pending\\n  logout/account-switch scenarios.\\n- Correct an existing target-creation test to assert the ingestion panel's status;\\n  its whole-page count failed identically on unchanged main because the workspace\\n  legitimately shows the same badge in two places.\\n\\nNo backend context cache, provider history, dependency, migration or environment\\nchange. S2-010/011 service contracts, token budget, quota and disabled-by-default\\nconfiguration are preserved.\\n\\n## Validation\\n\\n- 80 Jest suites / 1,473 tests passed, zero skips.\\n- 31/31 production-build Edge browser tests passed with local fake Supabase.\\n- Formatting, lint, explicit typecheck and production build passed.\\n- Changed-code coverage: 32/32 lines and 7/7 branch outcomes.\\n- Three restored source-fault challenges were caught by regression tests.\\n- Separate unpublished 006/017/019 composition typechecked; 47 focused tests passed.\\n  Shared workspace reconciliation must retain userId={user.id} on ReviewQuestions.\\n\\nNo paid provider calls or hosted database changes. Browser answers are intercepted;\\nprovider-boundary tests use the real adapter over fake HTTP with synthetic config.\\nThis does not certify live model semantics or hosted RLS deployment. Important: PR 80 / S2-006 must merge first. \\r\\nS2-017: Switch between owned analysis targets with clear active context\\r\\n\\r\\n## PR description\\r\\n\\r\\nCloses #68.\\r\\n\\r\\nAdds a **Switch target** control to the workspace header. Users can navigate directly to another\\r\\nowned target, see which target is active, and distinguish duplicate target names by their saved\\r\\nsource URLs. The active identity remains clear in the workspace, ingestion card, and Q&A.\\r\\nSwitching uses the existing target URLs, preserving refresh and browser history behavior.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe switcher supports keyboard navigation and Escape dismissal with focus restoration. Empty\\r\\nand failed switch lists explain the next step without discarding the current workspace.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Security and context isolation\\r\\n\\r\\n- Authenticate and verify target ownership before loading the switch list or child data.\\r\\n- Reuse the owner-filtered list and session-scoped Supabase client; send only ID, name, and\\r\\n  source URL to the switcher. Missing and foreign targets remain indistinguishable.\\r\\n- Render names and sources as escaped text; switch links use fixed internal routes and encoded\\r\\n  target IDs.\\r\\n- Preserve existing target-scoped Q&A context rebuilding, form reset, pending-request\\r\\n  cancellation, response-target validation, and empty-dataset restrictions.\\r\\n\\r\\nNo schema, RLS, grants, dependency versions, or provider integrations changed. The existing\\r\\n200-target list limit remains.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Test evidence\\r\\n\\r\\n- Lint, formatting, typecheck, migration filename validation, and production build passed.\\r\\n- Full Jest run: **80 suites / 1,468 tests passed**.\\r\\n- Changed-code coverage against the starting branch HEAD: **100% (112/112 lines)**.\\r\\n- Production Playwright: **14/14 passed** across target switching, workspace, and Q&A, including\\r\\n  keyboard/Escape controls, refresh/Back, logout, cross-user denial, and 375px layout.\\r\\n- Added regression tests for duplicate names, escaped untrusted text, safe list-load failures,\\r\\n  and authentication/ownership ordering. Inspected desktop/mobile screenshots.\\r\\n\\r\\nTests use deterministic fixtures and mocked model responses for UI assertions. Live hosted\\r\\nSupabase/RLS and live-model evaluation were not run for this UI change.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Review and AI assistance\\r\\n\\r\\nAI assistance: **OpenAI Codex**, author **melissa**. Local functionality/security review found and\\r\\nfixed duplicate-name ambiguity and keyboard dismissal/focus behavior. Independent teammate and\\r\\nsecurity review are still required before merge.\\r\\n\\r\\n- [Implementation and verification](docs/s2-017-switch-active-target.md)\\r\\n- [Functionality/security review](docs/s2-017-code-security-review.md)\\r\\n- [Implementation transcript](ai-transcripts/2026-11-15_melissa_S2-017_codex.md)\\r\\n- [Review transcript](ai-transcripts/2026-11-15_melissa_S2-017_review_codex.md)\\r\\n\",\"comments\":[]}"

returns a null average for empty datasets and distinguishes unavailable data from zero reviews.\\n- Integrates `ReviewSummaryCard` into `ProtectedTargetWorkspace` **after authentication and target ownership verification**.\\n- Handles summary-load errors independently with a safe message and retry while preserving the ingestion and review-list sections.\\n- Uses target-scoped server rendering so switching targets displays the correct summary; the existing ingestion completion `router.refresh()` updates the server-rendered workspace summary.\\n- Includes API, service, component, security regression, and workspace tests covering counts, averages, empty datasets, access control, failures, target switching, and refresh after ingestion.\\n\\n## Implementation\\n\\n- Workspace: `src/components/analysis-targets/ProtectedTargetWorkspace.tsx`\\n- Summary card: `src/components/reviews/ReviewSummaryCard.tsx`\\n- Summary service and persisted aggregate: `src/services/reviews/review-summary.ts` and `supabase/migrations/20261113000000_create_review_summary.sql`\\n- Authenticated route: `src/app/api/analysis-targets/[targetId]/review-summary/route.ts`\\n- Tests: `tests/unit/app/analysis-targets/workspace.test.tsx`, summary route/service/component tests, and `tests/db/review-summary.test.sql`\\n- Documentation: `docs/s2-006-review-summary.md` and the S2-006 AI transcript.\\n\\n## Validation\\n\\n- Latest Codex-reported run: **60 Jest suites, 854 tests passed**; ESLint, TypeScript, and Prettier passed.\\n- Contributor Mac validation previously confirmed production `npm run build` passed. Mac validation on the final workspace-refresh test revision is not independently verified in this PR description.\\n- Latest GitHub CI **passed**, including the SQL authorization tests for the new PostgreSQL aggregate (confirmed in Shawn's review at `533b78f`).\\n- Deployment of the migration to shared Supabase remains **unconfirmed**; passing CI is not evidence that it has been deployed.\\n\\n## Reviewer notes\\n\\n- **Melissa:** Workspace integration is now included in this PR, including summary failure handling, target switching, and refresh-after-ingestion test coverage.\\n- **Shawn:** Independent security review **APPROVED** at `533b78f`; 58 focused tests passed. No blocking findings.\\n- **Merge readiness:** Both functional and security reviews are approved; CI passed and the PR is mergeable. No further code fixes are currently required.\\n- **Deployment coordination required:** Before the updated application uses the summary endpoint, the PM/database owner must confirm the responsible migration deployer and deployment time, check prerequisites, apply `supabase/migrations/20261113000000_create_review_summary.sql` through the team process, and verify owned/empty/unauthorized target behavior. Record the responsible person, environment, scheduled time, and verification results in this PR.     "text": "{\"number\":80,\"head\":\"404429d4d21031357de6b066b550f433850bc4d9\",\"merged\":true,\"draft\":false,\"mergeable\":false,\"body\":\"## Summary\\n\\nImplements Sprint 2 **S2-006 — Display Review Count and Rating Summary**.\\n\\n- Adds an authenticated, owner-scoped review summary service and GET endpoint for the selected AnalysisTarget.\\n- Uses the PostgreSQL `get_review_summary` aggregate (`SECURITY INVOKER`) to calculate exact persisted review count and average rating without loading review text, reviewer names, or other full review rows for the metrics.\\n- Includes persisted ingestion status and skipped count; returns a null average for empty datasets and distinguishes unavailable data from zero reviews.\\n- Integrates `ReviewSummaryCard` into `ProtectedTargetWorkspace` **after authentication and target ownership verification**.\\n- Handles summary-load errors independently with a safe message and retry while preserving the ingestion and review-list sections.\\n- Uses target-scoped server rendering so switching targets displays the correct summary; the existing ingestion completion `router.refresh()` updates the server-rendered workspace summary.\\n- Includes API, service, component, security regression, and workspace tests covering counts, averages, empty datasets, access control, failures, target switching, and refresh after ingestion.\\n\\n## Implementation\\n\\n- Workspace: `src/components/analysis-targets/ProtectedTargetWorkspace.tsx`\\n- Summary card: `src/components/reviews/ReviewSummaryCard.tsx`\\n- Summary service and persisted aggregate: `src/services/reviews/review-summary.ts` and `supabase/migrations/20261113000000_create_review_summary.sql`\\n- Authenticated route: `src/app/api/analysis-targets/[targetId]/review-summary/route.ts`\\n- Tests: `tests/unit/app/analysis-targets/workspace.test.tsx`, summary route/service/component tests, and `tests/db/review-summary.test.sql`\\n- Documentation: `docs/s2-006-review-summary.md` and the S2-006 AI transcript.\\n\\n## Validation\\n\\n- Final Mac validation after merging the current main into S2-006: **85 Jest suites, 1,540 tests passed**, ESLint, TypeScript and production build passed.\\n- Latest GitHub CI on `404429d` **passed**, including changed-code coverage and database/migration checks, as confirmed by the renewed reviews.\\n- Deployment to shared Supabase remains **unconfirmed**; passing CI does not establish deployment.\\n\\n## Reviewer notes\\n\\n- **Melissa:** Functional **APPROVED** at `404429d`; S2-006 summary and S2-009 Q&A both preserved through workspace conflict resolution, with fresh CI and tests passing.\\n- **Shawn:** Security **APPROVED** at `404429d`; 1,540 tests, lint/typecheck and both CI jobs verified; no remaining security blocker.\\n- **Merge readiness:** Latest head `404429d` is non-Draft, conflict-free, approved and CI-green.\\n- **Deployment handoff:** Aryan Modi agreed to apply `supabase/migrations/20261113000000_create_review_summary.sql` to shared Supabase together with S2-009's quota migration after PR #80 and PR #84 have merged. PR #84 is already merged. The exact deployment time and completion/verification are **not yet confirmed**. Coordinate application rollout so the summary endpoint is not used before migration. Aryan should document the target environment, application time, prerequisite migrations and checks of owned/empty/unauthorized target behavior after applying it; keep `QA_ENABLED=false` until quota migration is deployed and approved.\\n\\n**Closes #57**\",\"comments\":[{\"body\":\"Hey Bansi, please include the workspace integration in PR #80 so S2-006 delivers the complete user-facing feature. S2-005 handles ingestion status; it doesn’t display the new review summary card.\\r\\n\\r\\nPlease load the active target’s persisted summary, render `ReviewSummaryCard` in the workspace, handle load failures, and verify it refreshes after ingestion and shows the correct data when switching targets. Include workspace tests for those behaviors.\\r\\n\\r\\nWith integration included, “Closes #57” can stay. Please also address the component compatibility issue, endpoint tests, and Shawn’s security feedback, then rerun CI against current `main`. Thanks!\",\"user\":\"ms-00star\"},{\"body\":\"S2-006 PR #80 at 6cb597b: changes requested before security approval.\\n\\nOne finding: the summary service loads every full review into server memory to calculate count and average. This includes unnecessary review text, reviewer names, and metadata, with no total read bound.\\n\\nRequired fix: use an owner-authorized database aggregate returning count and average. Preserve accurate totals—don’t silently truncate reviews. With Cache Components, Next.js preserves state and DOM out of the box.\\r\\n\\r\\nInstead of unmounting pages on navigation, Next.js hides them using React's [`<Activity>`](https://react.dev/reference/react/Activity) component. Activity keeps the DOM in the document (hidden with `display: none`), so both React state and DOM state are preserved: form drafts, scroll positions, expanded `<details>` elements, video playback progress, and more.\\r\\n\\r\\nNext.js preserves up to 3 routes. Beyond that, the oldest route is evicted and will re-render fresh.\\r\\n\\r\\n> **Good to know:** Use [`useRouter().bfcacheId`](/docs/app/api-reference/functions/use-router#bfcacheid) as a [React `key`](https://react.dev/learn/preserving-and-resetting-state#option-2-resetting-state-with-a-key): a single `<Fragment key={bfcacheId}>` resets an entire subtree on push or replace navigations (including `<Link>` clicks and `router.push` / `router.replace`) while still restoring state on browser back/forward. `bfcacheId` is mainly a migration tool. For new code, prefer the per-pattern resets below.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Choosing what to preserve\\r\\n\\r\\nActivity preserves all component state and DOM state by default. text(await tools.exec_command({cmd:"$code = @'\nconst fs=require('fs');let s=fs.readFileSync('../recover-s2-019-transcript.cjs','utf8');s=s.slice(0,s.indexOf('const stats=[...turns.values()]'));s=s.replace('new Set([55,164,165,166,173,174,175,176,177,178,179])','new Set([1,2,3,21,22,130,131,133,160,163,167,173,190,191])');\ns+=`const stats=[...turns.values()], messages=stats.reduce((n,s)=>n+s.user+s.assistant,0),tools=stats.reduce((n,s)=>n+s.tools,0),cutoff=entries.at(-1)?.timestamp;\nlet out='# S2-018 - Recovered original Codex conversation\\\\n\\\\nAuthor: Shawn. Tool: OpenAI Codex in VS Code. Issue #69.\\\\n\\\\n## Scope and provenance\\\\n\\\\nRecovered from the retained primary direct session, not reconstructed from summaries. All available user/assistant messages and tool records in the selected story-related turns are retained in order with privacy redactions. These cover initial work, implementation, summary dependency discussion, draft publication, shared 018/019 dependency status, and the current completion request. Intervening unrelated-story work is excluded. Companion-page control messages associated with initial work are retained; no attachment content is invented. Older summaries remain clearly historical and are not substitutes for this original dialogue.\\\\n\\\\nSelected source turns: '+stats.map(s=>s.turn).join(', ')+'. Original user/assistant messages: '+messages+'. Tool records: '+tools+'. Snapshot cutoff: '+cutoff+'. Source snapshot bytes: '+snapshot+'. Later tool results do not exist yet at the cutoff and are not invented. System/developer content, hidden reasoning, compaction summaries, binary attachments, and internal approval-reviewer records are excluded. Paths, non-fixture emails, provider-key patterns and JWTs are redacted. No claim is made to export the unrelated entire project history.\\\\n\\\\n## Accepted, changed, rejected\\\\n\\\\nAccepted: real production context builder and Q&A adapter, persisted summary service/route integration, ownership checks before child reads, explicit active-target labels, current-main 006/019 integration, browser and mutation checks. Changed: restore summary checks, scope Q&A alerts separately from ingestion failures, use distinct sequential questions, and recover original dialogue instead of relying on summaries. Rejected: a second context cache/coordinator, copying unmerged selector/UI production code, paid provider calls, hosted changes, and unapproved GitHub mutations.\\\\n\\\\n## Original records\\\\n';\nfor(const e of entries){out+='\\\\n### '+e.timestamp+' - '+e.kind+' (source turn '+e.turn+', record '+e.line+')\\\\n\\\\n';if(e.kind==='user'||e.kind==='assistant')out+=e.text+'\\\\n';else{const runs=e.text.match(/\\x60+/g)||[],fence='\\x60'.repeat(Math.max(3,...runs.map(x=>x.length+1)));out+=fence+'text\\\\n'+e.text+'\\\\n'+fence+'\\\\n';}}\nconst audit={sourceKind:'primary-vscode-direct-session',sourceSnapshotBytes:snapshot,cutoff,selectedTurns:stats,originalUserAssistantMessages:messages,toolRecords:tools,parseErrors,outputBytes:Buffer.byteLength(out),outputSha256:crypto.createHash('sha256').update(out).digest('hex')};fs.writeFileSync('ai-transcripts/2026-11-15_shawn_S2-018_recovered_codex.md',out);fs.writeFileSync('../s2-018-transcript-recovery-audit-2026-11-15.json',JSON.stringify(audit,null,2));console.log(JSON.stringify({messages,tools,cutoff,bytes:audit.outputBytes,parseErrors}));})();`;\nfs.writeFileSync('../recover-s2-018-transcript.cjs',s);\n'@; $code | node; node ../recover-s2-018-transcript.cjs","workdir":"<USER_HOME>\\Documents\\NJIT\\Fall 26\\CS490\\ReviewLens-Codex-Artifacts\\reviewlens-s2-018-pr","sandbox_permissions":"require_escalated","yield_time_ms":1000,"max_output_tokens":1000}));

An ingestion failure with saved reviews does not disable Q&A;\na genuinely empty dataset does. A failed summary read displays a safe error rather\nthan stale metrics or a fabricated zero.\n\n## Security boundaries\n\n- Identity comes from the verified session; parent ownership is checked before\n  child data and rechecked in the context/summary services.\n- Questions contain only the question; browser evidence and owner claims are not\n  accepted as persisted context. Every question rebuilds context from saved rows.\n- Complete counting/generation requests use only the selected target's evidence;\n  previous questions are excluded.
